5438               	;
5439               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
5440               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
5441               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
5442               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
5443               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
5444               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
5445               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
5446               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
5447               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
5448               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
5449               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
5450               	; 	'
5451               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
5452               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
5453               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
5454               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
5455               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.
5456               	; try_esc:
5457               cvb_TRY_ESC
5458               	; 	IF klv = 3 THEN RETURN			' the roof climbs nowhere

6505               	;
6506               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
6507               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
6508               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
6509               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
6510               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
6511               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
6512               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
6513               	; 	'
6514               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
6515               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
6516               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
6517               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
6518               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
6519               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
6520               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
6521               	; 	hacc = hacc + hsp4

6810 CDE6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
6811 CDE8 0450  20 	b *r0
6812               	;
6813               	; 	' ======================================================================
6814               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
6815               	; 	' ======================================================================
6816               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
6817               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
6818               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
6819               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
6820               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
6821               	; 	'
6822               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
6823               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
6824               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
6825               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
6826               	; 	' broken.
6827               	; 	'
6828               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
6829               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
6830               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
6831               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
6832               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
6833               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
6834               	; 	'
6835               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
6836               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
6837               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
6838               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
6839               	; coll_obst:
6840               cvb_COLL_OBST
6841               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
6842 CDEA D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     CDEC 215A

     CDF0 0300

7356               	; 	' KELLY FLASHES WHILE HE IS KNOCKED ABOUT. Hitting an obstacle set a
7357               	; 	' 20-frame `knock` and took nine seconds off the clock, and NOTHING on
7358               	; 	' screen said so -- the time simply went. A flash is the arcade's own
7359               	; 	' idiom for "that hit you", costs one variable, and is readable even

7384               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
7385               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
7386               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
7387               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
7388               	; 	'
7389               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
7390               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
7391               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
7392               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
7393               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
7394               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
7395               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.
7396               	; 	ky = flry(klv)

8091               	;
8092               	; 	' ======================================================================
8093               	; 	' THE SCANNER
8094               	; 	' ======================================================================
8095               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
8096               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
8097               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
8098               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
8099               	; 	'
8100               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
8101               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
8102               	; 	'
8103               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
8104               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
8105               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
8106               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
8107               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
8108               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
8109               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
8110               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
8111               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
8112               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
8113               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
8114               	; esc_tick:
8115               cvb_ESC_TICK
8116               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
8117               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
8118               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
8119               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
8120               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
8121 D8DA D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     D8DC 2149     
8122 D8DE 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     D8E0 0100     
8123 D8E2 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
8124 D8E4 1402  14 	jhe cv612
8125 D8E6 0460  28 	b @cv611
     D8E8 D8FE     
8126               cv612
8127               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
8128 D8EA D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     D8EC 2149     
8129 D8EE 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     D8F0 0700     
8130 D8F2 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
8131 D8F4 1A02  14 	jl cv614
8132 D8F6 0460  28 	b @cv613
     D8F8 D8FE     
8133               cv614
8134 D8FA C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8135 D8FC 0450  20 	b *r0
8136               cv613
8137               	; 	END IF
8138               cv611
8139               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
8140               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
8141               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
8142               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
8143               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
8144               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
8145               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
8146               	; 	'
8147               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
8148               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
8149               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
8150               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
8151               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
8152               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
8153               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
8154               	; 	escp = escp + 1
8155 D8FE D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     D900 20AF

     D904 0100

8372               	;
8373               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
8374               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
8375               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
8376               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
8377               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
8378               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
8379               	; 	'
8380               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
8381               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
8382               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
8383               	; 	'
8384               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
8385               	; 	'   +1  BLACK   Kelly -- and the escalator's FOOT, which the manual
8386               	; 	'               draws black anyway, so the diagonal spans both rows
8387               	; 	'   +2  WHITE   Harry
8388               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line
8389               	; 	'
8390               	; 	' Four is the floor: the Kop, the crook, the furniture and the floor
8391               	; 	' lines are four different colours and none of them can share. Shrinking
8392               	; 	' from six bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the instrument and
8393               	; 	' four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor above it or
8394               	; 	' the bottom of the screen.
8395               	; 	'
8396               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
8397               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
8398               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.

8618               	; 	' THE ELEVATOR MARKER TRACKS THE CAR, which is the whole point of it. It
8619               	; 	' used to be a static bar on all three shopping floors -- that says where
8620               	; 	' the shaft is, which never changes and which the player already knows,
8621               	; 	' and says nothing about the one fact that matters: whether the car is
8622               	; 	' where you are. Erase, move, redraw, exactly like an actor's dot.
8623               	; 	' ONE ROW NOW, AND WIDER FOR IT. The band is four pixel rows and each
8624               	; 	' one carries a single colour, so the car's grey row is the same row the
8625               	; 	' escalator heads use; spending a second row on it would put half the
8626               	; 	' car on Kelly's BLACK row, where it would read as the Kop. Five pixels
8627               	; 	' across at a fixed centre column tells it from a three-pixel actor dot

9135               	; 	' used as the shift directly. It was used directly, which MIRRORED every

9137               	; 	' as the radar being approximate rather than as being wrong.

9781 E608 0460  28 	b @cvb_MAIN
     E60A AA0A     
9782               	;
9783               	; 	' REPEATED ADDITION, not a multiply, and deliberately so. Two separate
9784               	; 	' hazards meet on this one line: `tsec * bmul.` is invalid because the `.`
9785               	; 	' suffix marks a 16-bit CONSTANT and both of these are variables, and a
9786               	; 	' plain `tsec * bmul` would be an 8-bit product -- a 50-second capture in
9787               	; 	' the x300 band pays 15,000, which is six times past a byte. Multiplying
9788               	; 	' properly would then land on the MPY/r0 hazard, where a 16-bit var read
9789               	; 	' straight after being multiplied returns the product's HIGH word.
9790               	; 	'
9791               	; 	' At most 50 adds, once per capture. Nothing about this is a hot path.
9792               	; 	'
9793               	; 	' The guard matters: a COMPUTED `FOR 1 TO 0` still runs its body once in
9794               	; 	' CVBasic, so catching Harry on the very last tick would pay a full
9795               	; 	' multiplier instead of nothing.
9796               	; calc_bonus:
9797               cvb_CALC_BONUS
9798               	; 	#bonus = 0

     E60E 20D4

     E616 0100

     E61E E654     
9806               cv734

     E622 0100     
9809 E624 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_BI
     E626 2146     
9810               cv735
9811               	; 			#bonus = #bonus + bmul

     E62A 2083

     E632 20D4

     E638 20D4

10032               	;
10033               	; 	' ======================================================================
10034               	; 	' SOUND
10035               	; 	' ======================================================================
10036               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
10037               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
10038               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
10039               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
10040               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.
10041               	; sfx_tick:

10389               	;
10390               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
10391               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
10392               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
10393               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
10394               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
10395               	; 	'
10396               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
10397               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
10398               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
10399               	; 	'
10400               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
10401               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
10402               	; 	' letter after it.
10403               	;
10404               	; font_bits:
10405               cvb_FONT_BITS
10406               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space
10407 EB2A 0000     	byte >00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00
     EB2C 0000

10524               	;
10525               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
10526               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
10527               	; 	'
10528               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
10529               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
10530               	; 	' being small sprites.
10531               	; 	'
10532               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
10533               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
10534               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
10535               	; 	'
10536               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
10537               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
10538               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10539               	;
10540               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly RIGHT then LEFT: hat, face, tunic, leg1, leg2, duck-hat, duck-face. Patterns 0..52
10541               cvb_SPR_KELLY
10542               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00

11191               	;
11192               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
11193               	; 	' Chars 160-207: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
11194               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
11195               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
11196               	;
11197               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
11198               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
11199               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
11200               	; 	' unreadable.
11201               	;
11202               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then furniture grey / Kelly black / Harry white / floor line yellow per 4 px level, on dark green
11203               cvb_SCAN_COL3
11204               	; 	DATA BYTE $EE,$EE,$EE,$EE,$EC,$1C,$FC,$BC
11205 F692 EEEE     	byte >ee,>ee,>ee,>ee,>ec,>1c,>fc,>bc
     F694 EEEE     
     F696 EC1C     
     F698 FCBC     
11206               	; 	DATA BYTE $EC,$1C,$FC,$BC,$EC,$1C,$FC,$BC
11207 F69A EC1C     	byte >ec,>1c,>fc,>bc,>ec,>1c,>fc,>bc
     F69C FCBC     
     F69E EC1C     
     F6A0 FCBC     
11208               	; 	DATA BYTE $EC,$1C,$FC,$BC,$EE,$EE,$EE,$EE
11209 F6A2 EC1C     	byte >ec,>1c,>fc,>bc,>ee,>ee,>ee,>ee
     F6A4 FCBC     
     F6A6 EEEE     
     F6A8 EEEE

11273               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
11274               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
11275               	; 	'
11276               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
11277               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
11278               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
11279               	; 	'
11280               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
11281               	; 	' blit is
11282               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
11283               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
11284               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
11285               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
11286               	; 	'
11287               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
11288               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
11289               	; 	'
11290               	; 	' template ids:
11291               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
11292               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
11293               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
11294               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
11295               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
11296               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
11297               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
11298               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
11299               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
11300               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
11301               	;
11302               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
11303               cvb_STOR_TPL
